# STATE-VAULT.md: the task ledger

One list of tasks for the vault work and the repository around it. Status is `todo | in-progress | in-review | done | blocked`. Evidence points to a command and its result, a pull request, a CI run or a transaction. Update the row in the pull request that does the work. Days are in BRT unless a row says otherwise.

## The build

The days and milestones are in `PLAN-VAULT.md`. A slot is about half a day of one stream. "Check" is what proves the row; section numbers, the hostile cases (A1 to A18) and the invariants (I1 to I5) are in `DESIGN-VAULT.md`. Owner "Thom" or "Rodrigo" means that person's sessions; either can take a row by writing their name in it first.

CI on the branch: `ci` and `program` green. `basket.so` is 460,600 bytes, `mock_router.so` 275,224. Fix round after the security review, same day. (1) The first hook check never fired on the real stock mint: the token crate (`spl-token-2022` 6.0.0) stops at extension types 25 and 26, which the stock tokens carry ahead of their hook, and the check read that error as no hook. `upsert_asset` now walks the extension list by hand, refuses a list it cannot read to its end, and is tested on SPYx's own bytes with and without a hook program. (2) A version number is never used twice: `Recipe.last_version`, four of the reserved bytes, with the size and every other offset unchanged. (3) `init_router` of the test exchange takes only the program's upgrade authority. (4) The publish delay is at most 30 days and the cooldown at most 7; the cash mint comes in as an account and has to be a mint (`set_cash_mint` is its own instruction); the replay reads which program refused. (5) `staging` merged: the six errors this slot appended (6028 to 6033) are in the shared `PROGRAM_ERRORS` and `CHAIN_ERROR_RETRYABLE`. Sections 3.7 and 6 of the design and `programs/README.md` follow the program. One chain per order (`ONE-CHAIN`): a buy names no chain and is on the person's, and a request that sends `chains` is refused with a sentence; a plan made for another chain or with recipes on several is refused; an order stored with steps on two chains answers 409 on every route and nothing is written; `Order` refuses legs on two chains for anything but a publish; the portfolio and the funding read are for that chain; an order on one chain with a failed leg is `failed`. The picked chain (`CHAIN-PICK`): `users.chain_id`, `GET /v1/me`, `PUT /v1/me/chain`, written once; an outside wallet names the chain of its family, an EVM wallet meaning Robinhood Chain, and that chain is stored the first time it is seen, so a wallet linked later does not move the person. A buy when the plan keeps cash: the whole amount is deposited (`Order.depositRaw` once; `Leg.cashRaw`, `legs.cash_raw` on the approval and on the deposit), the trades spend the invested share, tested at 10,000, at 9,500 and all cash on both chain families; a shared portfolio held as one line is flattened first, and a plan whose lines no longer match is refused (`VERSION_CHANGED`). Report matching on EVM: an attempt is the pair (message, nonce) with the nonce read by `nonceOf` at report time and stored; after cancel-then-rebuild and after revert-then-rebuild the right attempt settles; two orders of one wallet are not built on the same nonce (409 with `details.blocking`, and a lock for two builds at once); a transaction on a nonce below every nonce the step stated, an older identical call, settles nothing; signed bytes are not relayed for an order that has expired. Funding: `GET /v1/funding`, from the adapter's `funding` read, each figure with source, time, method and provenance; the query may name the wallet to read, one of the person's. Limits: `LIMITS` in `plugins/limits.ts` (60 a minute anonymous, 120 signed in, 30 on the builders, 10 kept for the parser), a failed sign-in counted; default deny (the API does not start with a `/v1` route that declares no sign-in rule or no limit class, or with one registered outside the `/v1` scope, and a route with no rule is closed whatever its method); CORS by path as the router reads it, `/v1` on the `CORS_ORIGINS` allowlist. The OpenAPI document of `/v1` at `packages/sdk/openapi.json`, with the two tokens as security schemes and each route's rule and limit class, and a test that fails when routes and file differ. The row's two checks. No registered route reaches a signer: `sign.ts` and `wallet.ts` are behind `@colosseum/chain-solana/server`; the one route that signs on the server, `POST /policies/:id/rebalance`, is in `routes/monitor-rebalance.ts`, loaded only by a dynamic import inside `if (flags.legacyStructurer)`, and the flag's default is now off; `tests/boundaries.test.ts` (19 tests) has no exemption left, holds that import to that `if`, walks every entry in a package's `exports`, names the modules and functions that turn a raw key into a signer, and fails if anything an app loads at start reaches a signing entry; `apps/api/src/legacy.test.ts` shows the rebalance absent with the flag off and present with it on, and the monitor's four other routes served either way. An unrelated transaction cannot confirm a leg, on both chain families: the same wallet's other call, another wallet's transaction for the same step, an id the chain has not seen, bytes that are not the step's, and on EVM another order's identical call on the nonce that order stated. Each rule was removed in turn, 61 of them, and a test failed for each. The risk layer's routes: `tests/risk-routes-untouched.test.ts` holds the API's `/risk/*` to what `apps/risk-api` answers (no sign-in, no limit, any origin reflected, the same documents); `apps/risk-api` and the `/risk` route files were not edited. What a client sees differently: `chains` on a buy is refused (400); a buy and the portfolio are on one chain; a person who made their wallets in the app gets 409 until they pick; `Order.depositRaw` and `Leg.cashRaw`; a plan under 10,000 bps is bought, where it was refused; on EVM a second order waits for the first one's open transaction; `POST /policies/:id/rebalance` is not served unless `LEGACY_STRUCTURER=on`; signed bytes for an expired order answer 410; a `/v1` route with no rule is closed for a GET too; 429 over the limits; `/v1` answers a browser only from `CORS_ORIGINS`. Not in this slot, and still open before a chain is `live`: `apps/api/src/orders/README.md`. `scripts/archive` still imports the three signing names from the root entry and may not be edited by an agent, so the root typecheck leaves that folder out until a person changes four static imports and one dynamic import | API-1 |

| HERO-HANDOVER | Oct 6 | The hero's joint was cut flat by the showcase where the two meet (Thom's screenshot at 1365 by 836) | Thom | in-review | Branch `web/hero-clip-fix`. **Cause:** the pinned layer was pulled up under the copy with a negative bottom margin, so its margin box had no height and the pin held until the stage's foot reached the top of the window: for a whole screen the showcase, which has its own ground, rose over the seated joint and cut it at a hard line. It has been so since WEB-2b; the ink joint of JOINT-3D made it read as broken. **Now:** the layer keeps its own screen of the stage's height and the copy is pulled up over it, so the pin lets go when the stage's foot reaches the window's foot; the joint, seated, fades over the last quarter screen before that (`FADE`, `shownAt`), so it is whole on the screen or not there, and never rides into the bar's band either. The space after step 03 goes from 45vh to 55vh so the seated joint is held before it fades. The closing's coins have their own renderer, observer and listeners and share nothing with the hero's. Tests: `e2e/landing-handover.spec.ts` scrolls down through the hand-over and back at 1365, 1440 and 390 (whole while shown, seated before it goes, never under the showcase); two unit tests. | WEB-SIGNIN-SLOW | Oct 6 | Someone signed in is never left waiting with no word: after 15 s not ready, the bar and the goal's sheet say sign-in is slow and which side, with "Try again" and "Sign out" | Thom | in-review | Branch `web/account-chain-fix`, 2026-10-06. Cause of Thom's screen (the bar on "Your wallet", the sheet on "Reading where your plan lives…"), reproduced in a real sign-in on `staging` by holding one request: Privy's embedded-wallet frame (`auth.privy.io/apps/<app>/embedded-wallets`) did not load, so Privy said who is signed in and never handed over the wallets; our server is not asked in that state. Why the frame did not load in his tab is not known. `AccountProvider` gives `slow` (`wallets`: the sign-in service; `server`: GET /v1/me or the chain being stored) and `again()`; `features/account/SlowSignIn.tsx` says it in the account block and on the goal's sheet. "Try again" mounts the wallet provider again (`useWalletRestart`, no reload; the typed goal stays) or asks our server again, the second press both. Nothing is asked again by itself, and each press waits twice as long before the next is offered (15 s, 30 s, 60 s, 120 s), so a sign-in service that answered 429 is left alone. Tests: `slow-sign-in.events.test.ts` 9 (EN and PT: the wait, each side, the back-off, recovery; sign out; never for someone not known to be signed in), `screen-port.events.test.ts` +1 (the remount). In the same real sign-in with the frame held: the notice at 16 s, ready 4 s after "Try again", the goal still typed. Changed assertion: `product-routes.test.ts` opens `useWalletRestart` to `AccountProvider.tsx` alone. Bite checks, each restored: no doubling, no remount, the bar dropping its control, the goal or its draft forgotten on restart, our server not asked again: each fails a test. After `/review-pr 151`: on restart the port of the provider that is gone is swapped for a loading one that keeps the person's id, so no signer of unmounted hooks is handed out and our server is asked only with the new provider's tokens; a restart is refused while an order is being run (`useSigningHold` in the runner; the notice says to finish or cancel the step first); the calls that make wallets go one at a time across mounts (the provider keeps the queue; a driver left behind stops before its next wallet; the next waits for a call still open, 30 s at most); a sign-in open on the old port is rejected. Tests: `privy-bridge.events.test.ts` +3, `screen-port.events.test.ts` 2 (the one that held the old port in place now expects it gone), `slow-sign-in.events.test.ts` +3 cases (refused press, another person and signed out after a restart), `order-screen.events.test.ts` +1. Changed assertions: `product-routes.test.ts` opens `useSigningHold` to the runner and reads its dependency list as `[port, apiFetch, hold]`. Seen in the real sign-in, pressing "Try again" with the frame still held: Privy aborts the open frame load and removes the frame, the new provider makes one frame and asks for its app settings and session again, no page error; ready 4 s after the frame was let through. Not seen: a `createWallet` open at the unmount, which needs a new account | |
| WEB-BAR-NEVER-EMPTY | Oct 7 | The bar always has a way in: a visitor gets "Sign in" after 4 s whatever the sign-in service does, and the screen it opens says so when the service has not answered | Thom | in-review | Branch `web/bar-never-empty`, 2026-10-07. Thom's screen (Brave, `localhost:3000`, signed out): the bar kept an empty box where "Sign in" belongs, because it drew nothing until the sign-in service had loaded, with no limit. `AccountProvider` gives `stalled` after `WAY_IN_MS` (4 s) with the port loading and nobody known; the bar then draws the visitor's controls (the chain switcher, "Sign in"); the sign-in screen shows `SignInSilent` in place of "Loading sign-in…" (the service has not answered, offline or a blocker, "Try again" which mounts the wallet provider again); after 15 s the bar says "Sign-in is slow" beside the way in and the phone's sheet says which side (`service`). Tests: `bar-never-empty.events.test.ts` 14 (EN and PT). Changed assertion: `slow-sign-in.events.test.ts`, the case that expected nothing for someone not known to be signed in now expects the way in. Cause, from a probe on this branch's own dev server (each Privy request blocked in turn; Chromium, and Brave with default shields): only the app settings call (`GET auth.privy.io/api/v1/apps/<id>`) keeps Privy from becoming ready when it fails or hangs; the wallet frame, the session call and third-party cookies do not. And on `http://localhost:3000` the Privy app now refuses the origin: its API answers 403 `invalid_origin` and the wallet frame is refused by `frame-ancestors 'self' https://www.tenonfi.xyz https://tenonfi.xyz https://auth.privy.io`, so nobody can sign in on localhost and a session kept there cannot be refreshed. **Needs a person:** add `http://localhost:3000` to the Privy app's allowed origins, or give local development an app of its own. Privy's own `invalid_origin` on a press is said as "Sign-in isn’t set up for this address" (EN and PT), and "Create a new passkey" is not offered after it. An up-front read of the app's public `allowed_domains` was built and taken out after review: the field is not a documented contract, and an entry that is not an exact origin (a wildcard, a bare host) would have refused a good address. After `/review-pr 170`: someone the signed-in hint (`tf-in`) says was signed in is never shown "Sign in" while the service loads: the neutral box until 15 s, then "Sign-in is slow" with "Try again" and "Sign out"; that "Sign out", with the service naming nobody, forgets the hint and what this browser kept and gives the visitor's way in at once. Someone signed in whose provider, started again, names nobody keeps the account control. Tests +6 (20 in the file); the hint, the way out and `seen` each fail tests when removed. That sign-out is finished when the service loads, and the window before it is closed at the port (second review): the press leaves a mark (`tf-left`) and forgets every `tf-order:*` record; while the mark is set the real `WalletProvider` hands no consumer a port that names a person (every screen and the signing port see the wallet loading, nobody, no token, and `sign` rejects), asks the service to sign them out, again after a refusal with each wait twice the one before (2 s to a minute), and lets go of the mark when the service says nobody is signed in. Tests: `left-here.events.test.ts` 5, on the real provider with the order, withdrawal and vault screens mounted in that window (no order drawn, no button, no request with tokens, no owner links). Changed assertion: `product-routes.test.ts` opens `useLeaveHere` to `AccountProvider.tsx`. Handing the port out, dropping the back-off and keeping the mark each fail tests. Follow-ups (branch `web/left-here-followups`): a second tab learns of the mark at once (a `storage` listener), so a tab left open on the person's account is closed to them too; once the service says the person it named is out, the order records kept under their id since the press go (`useOustedPerson`), and no one else's; a sign-out that never answers counts as refused after 10 s and is tried again; and the bar, on the real provider through that window, shows "Sign in", no account and no signed-in hint. `left-here.events.test.ts` +5 (10). Changed assertion: `product-routes.test.ts` opens `useOustedPerson` to `AccountProvider.tsx`. Not verified: Thom's own browser profile | WEB-SIGNIN-SLOW |

Screens in `apps/web/features/shared/` (as built in `DESIGN-VAULT.md` section 11, "The shared-portfolio screens"): `/shelf` (in the bar), `/indexes/[slug]`, `/indexes/[slug]/buy`, `/publish`, `/vaults/[chain]/[address]`; publish, buy-to-follow and follow are orders signed on `/orders/[id]` through `execute`, the runner handed the screen's own terms (`terms.ts`, `planTermsOf`), never the API's order, and the order held to them before signing (`order-check.ts`). The family id is `familyIdOf(slug)` worked out on the page; the text is the form's. The gap API-3 reported is closed on Solana: a follow is held to the recipe the browser reads from this app's own node (`readSolanaRecipe`, new in `packages/sdk`, held to an account the program wrote in `tests/solana-vault/shared-routes.test.ts`); where it cannot read (the mock, no node set, no deployment, a portfolio not published through the app, EVM) the page says which and shows the server's answer as not checked; where it has a node and a deployment and the read fails (no answer, another account, another creator, a creator that is no Solana address), nothing is bought or followed and the page says the portfolio could not be verified and may have been tampered with (fix round of the #62 review). GOLD-ONE-TAP: the switch only where `autoFollow.offered`, the one-tap sentence and the reason otherwise. The follower's checklist of the #56 review: the recipe read from the app's own node at `recipeAddress(program, creator, familyIdOf(slug))`; `familyTextHash` of the text shown against the chain's versions, "can't vouch" otherwise; weights, version and creator from that read; every trade of a buy held to that version's assets and weights; `{ recipeOnchainId, version }` and `basketIdOfPlan(familyIdOf(slug))` of its own in the guard's terms; the platform badge only from `features/shared/platform.ts`; a portfolio whose id is not its slug's not offered (gate `FAMILY-ID`). Tests: `chain-read.events.test.ts` 3, `shared-screens.events.test.ts` 10; 3 more rules bitten. Also: the guard checks the mock's publish (`guard/mock/check.ts`, 6 negatives each refused by its own check), `GET /v1/vaults/{chain}/{address}` (public), `GET /v1/funding?family=`, the e2e stub serves the shared routes. | API-BUY-MIN | Oct 6 | Real buys on the hosted devnet failed at step 2: a step is built with the minimum its order stated, and a buy that stopped after its deposit can be finished with the cash in the vault | Thom | in-review | Branch `api/buy-min-pinned`, 2026-10-06. Cause (order 771a1fad on devnet): `buildFor` (`apps/api/src/orders/legs.ts`) handed the builders the trades and a slippage, `buildOwnerSwap` (`packages/chain-solana/src/vault/adapter.ts`) set the minimum from the route it took at that moment, and the guard (`packages/sdk/src/guard/run.ts`, the `preview` check) holds a built step to the minimum the person approved; thirty seconds of a yield token's price made them differ, after the deposit had landed. Fix: the builders of all three adapters take the order's stated minimums and write those (`statedMinimum`), and refuse with `PriceMoved` when a trade would now give less; the guard is unchanged. `POST /v1/orders/{id}/continue` finishes a stopped buy (`orders/continue.ts`): the swaps left, quoted now, no deposit, owner only, once per order. The mock keeps the stated minimums out of its bytes, where each floor is stated once (`mins`), so the mock guard's field list is as it was. Tests: the adapter contract (`packages/chain-mock/src/contract.ts`: the stated minimum to the unit at any slippage, `PriceMoved`, one per trade; it runs for the mock here and for the Solana and EVM adapters in their own jobs), `packages/sdk/src/executor/execute.test.ts` (a buy walked through the real guard after every price ticked; a price past the minimum signs nothing), `apps/api/src/routes/v1/order-terms.test.ts` (the same through HTTP; the continuation: owner only, no second deposit, the swaps' sum is the vault's cash, once per order). Bitten: without the pin in the mock the contract case fails four times, and without it in the API double the executor ends `refused` at step 2, as on devnet. The API tests need the database: CI runs them. Review round (Oct 6): the once-per-order rule could be raced, so recording a build and storing a continuation each take the order's lock in one short transaction that checks again and writes (`lockOrder`, an advisory lock of the transaction by the order's id, waited for two seconds at most, then 409 retryable), with tests that fire three continues at once and a build against a continue. The first form of it held a connection for the lock while the work ran on others, and across calls to the chain's node: ten requests at once took the pool of ten and nothing finished (the re-review's blocker). Now the node is asked before the transaction, a request holds one connection for a few statements, and `order-terms.test.ts` fires fifteen builds on fifteen orders, fifteen on one step and fifteen continues at once, each to answer within a bound and the database to answer after; a stated minimum of nothing is refused by `statedMinimum`, and an order whose trade quotes nothing is not made; `/continue` refuses a buy on an EVM chain, where the trades ride in the deposit and nothing is left in a vault; `continues` is no field of a request (kept in the row beside it, written from the order). EVM: the fork of Robinhood mainnet (`fork.test.ts`, reads) passes 31 of 31 from this machine; the builders' contract on a copy of the test network (`testnet.test.ts`) could not start here, the network's DNS refusing `rpc.testnet.chain.robinhood.com`, so the EVM builders' pin stands on the mock's EVM family and on reading the code. Codes (branch `api/continue-codes`, Oct 7): every refusal of `/continue` carries a code beside its sentence, so the web acts on the code: `ORDER_CONTINUED` (with `details.continuedBy`), `STEP_IN_FLIGHT` and `ORDER_BUSY` (both retryable), `STEP_LANDED` (read the order again), `NOTHING_LEFT`, `VAULT_CASH_SHORT`, `DEPOSIT_NOT_LANDED`, `CONTINUE_NOT_SUPPORTED`; a build refused for the same reason carries the same code; tests `orders/continue.test.ts` and `order-terms.test.ts`, the last also holding an id that is not one to 400 with `{ error }`. Orders already stopped on devnet are finished by the new route once deployed | — |
| DB-POOL | Oct 7 | The database client's pool is set from the environment, so the hosted API can sit behind Supabase's transaction pooler without a change of code | Thom | in-review | Branch `db/pool-config`, 2026-10-07. `packages/db/src/pool.ts`: `DB_POOL_MAX` (1 to 100) and `DB_PGBOUNCER=transaction`, which opens the driver with `prepare: false`; each is passed to the driver only when its variable is set, so with neither the address (`?max=`, `?prepare=false`) and the driver's own defaults decide as before. | API-PLANS | Oct 6 | A plan and its goal are kept on the server: the plan opens in a new tab, and the portfolio knows its goal and its history after signing in again (the flow audit's BROKEN finding) | Thom | in-review | Branch `api/plans-on-server`, 2026-10-06. No migration: `proposals` already stores the plan with its person, and the goal sheet is in the plan. API: `GET /v1/baskets/{id}` answers a plan to the person who made it (`loadReadablePlan`), with `fromLink` in the answer. The route stays public and reads a sign-in when one is sent (`config.optionalSignIn` in `plugins/auth.ts`, new: a public route knew nobody before); a token that is not valid is answered as nobody, never 401; another person, or nobody, gets the 404 an unknown id gets; a plan from a link is as before. `GET /v1/me/plans` (sign-in): the person's plans and the link plans they bought, each with its sheet, card, verdict, chain, buys (theirs by the wallets of the verified token, as an order is) and the vault's number; never another person's plan, also not one they bought by its id. Web: `use-plan.ts` reads the API when the tab has no plan and keeps the tab as a cache; the portfolio merges the server's list with what the browser kept (`server-plans.ts`), so the goal card, what was put in and "What was done" survive a new device; the card always leads to the plan. Not kept: the risk roll-up (a plan read back says so where the summary was), and orders about a shared portfolio (no goal; still the browser's). Fix round after review (Oct 6): `POST /v1/orders` and `GET /v1/funding` took any stored plan by its id, whoever made it; both now load with `loadBuyablePlan`, so a plan that names another person answers the 404 an unknown id does (a plan from a link is as before, and a row that names no person, stored before plans named theirs, is bought by its id as it was). Both reads send `Cache-Control: private, no-store`; a plan from a link with the agent surface off answers the same words as an unknown id; `PersonPlansResponse` is in `@colosseum/schemas`; `/v1/me/plans` is paged (follow-ups of the re-review, branch `api/plans-followups`: `limit` up to 50 and `before`, `next` in the answer, every buy of a plan on its page; the web follows up to four pages); the plan read is asked once more with fresh tokens before the screen says there is no plan, since the route answers stale tokens as it answers nobody; its 404 carries `Cache-Control: private, no-store` and `Vary: Authorization` as its 200 does; the route table's test holds `optionalSignIn` to that one route; the sign-in test names expired and wrong-audience tokens; the screens' sentences about plans kept in a tab and goals kept in a browser say what is true now. Tests: `plugins/auth.test.ts` (the optional sign-in: the person, nobody, a forged token, another issuer's), `plans-read.test.ts` (owner reads, another person and nobody get 404, agent surface off, the list before and after a buy, a link plan, a stranger's plan), `plan-buy.events.test.ts` (a new tab), `monitor-screen.events.test.ts` (a fresh sign-in, the link vault, no list), `server-plans.test.ts`. Changed assertions: `propose.test.ts` (the answer has `fromLink: true`; the 404's words), `tests/mcp-contract.test.ts` (the same words), `orders.test.ts` (the route table lists `/v1/me/plans`), `monitor-screen.events.test.ts` (the card leads to the plan, not the order). Runs paused by sleep took 16 to 94 minutes and found their block's state gone (`historical state … is not available`, `layer stale`); from 09:40 the network was unreachable. Fix on branch `risk-evm/resilience`: a token is measured again, prices included, on a fresh block when its block cannot be read; a run that has held its block over two minutes takes a fresh one for what is left; an outage, or a refused fresh block, is retried within the hour for the missing tokens, up to about minute 50; every scheduled hour gets a `slot` line in `runs.jsonl` with its rows and the reason for any gap. No fresh block is taken once the next run is due, so a run paused past its hour leaves the rest to the next one; after a late wake an hour is run only if half the interval is left, otherwise it is recorded as missed. `pnpm verify:quick` exits 0; `pnpm exec vitest run tests/risk-evm.test.ts` passes 86 tests; one real run on Oct 3 at block 79205979 wrote 21 rows in 23 s and 111 calls. | API-PROSE-FIGURE-TRIM | Oct 8 | A figure the model still writes in prose on its repair attempt costs that sentence, not the reply: the first real chat turn on the hosted API failed with `prose_figure` twice (new-goal conversation, robinhood) and the person got no answer | Thom | in-review | Branch `api/prose-figure-trim`, 2026-10-08. On the repair attempt only, `replyToVaultConversation` removes each sentence that states a figure (or "guaranteed", "risk-free") from `message`, `question`, `objective`, `summary`, each tradeoff, unknown and pick reason, and serves the rest; an empty question, tradeoff or unknown is dropped, an empty objective, summary or pick reason is one fixed server sentence (EN and PT), and an empty `message` is still refused with `prose_figure`. A full stop inside a catalog name, inside quotation marks or before a digit or a lower-case word does not split. Both routes log `detail: prose_figure_trimmed` with `sentencesCut` at warn, no words. The repair hint and the system instruction now say to write no digit and no percent or currency sign in prose and to cite the evidence id. Tests: `orders/vault-agent.test.ts`, `orders/vault-agent-requests.test.ts`, `vault-agent-model.test.ts` and the two conversation route tests, 452 passed locally without a database; 17 of them fail with the source change reversed. Assertions changed: a figure in an objective, a summary or a pick reason sent twice was a `prose_figure` refusal and is now a served reply without that sentence (`financial-figure` and `written-figure` left the refusal table and are cases of the new block; the quote, catalog-name and provider-fixture tests assert the trimmed reply); a message made only of figure sentences is still refused. Review of #197: a cut is no longer silent (the message ends with a fixed server sentence on a line of its own, and a tradeoff or unknown list that lost an item or part of one holds it once), a line break ends a sentence only after closing punctuation or before a list marker, and a field blanked whole counts in `sentencesCut`; the review's three inputs are cases in `vault-agent.test.ts`. Re-check of #197: a pass for negated caveats was added and removed again, since "not guaranteed by a bank but by the protocol" and the like promise; "guaranteed" and "risk-free" count as on staging, negated or not, and the re-check's sentences are cases. Assertions changed, all from this branch's first commit: a trimmed message now ends with the server sentence and a trimmed list holds it. Not verified against a live model or the hosted API | Live smoke |

| API-EVM-FUNDS-MIN | Oct 10 | Two follow-ups to `API-EVM-CHANGE-STEP` (#232). (1) Whether `firstShort` should count a sale's cash at its stated minimum and not its quote: not as orders are sized now. `rebalancePlan` sizes purchases from the reference prices less the order's slippage, and a sale's minimum is the pool's quote less that slippage, so a purchase that takes all its sale brings in is above the minimum by the pool's fee: held to the minimum, a change of one stock into half of another is refused before signing though it lands (`tests/evm-vault/funds-at-minimum.test.ts`, on the mock's 10 bps). The check stays at the quote, with the reason beside it; the fix is to size an order's purchases from its sales' stated minimums in `planRetarget`, and only then hold the step to them. Not done here: it changes what an order trades. (2) The Solana quote's cost was worked out with no check that it is a number (`referenceCost`, `packages/chain-solana/src/vault/adapter.ts`): a bought token's price that does not read as a number gave NaN in `Quote.costBps`, which an order's answer cannot carry. Both adapters now state it through one `referenceCostBps` (schemas), none where it is not finite | Thom; built by an agent | in-review | Branch `evm/funds-at-min-out`, 2026-10-10.
